Would you like me to show a **practical example** — like a dashboard layout with sidebar and nested routes that highlight the active link (using `NavLink`)?
That’s often asked in React interviews.

==========================================================================================================
React me useLocation ek hook hai jo React Router (specifically react-router-dom library) ke andar milta hai.
Ye hook current URL ki information (location object) return karta hai — jisme path, query parameters, hash, etc. hota hai.

## 🎯 Goal:

Hum ek page banayenge —
URL hoga:

```
http://localhost:3000/user?id=101&name=Vishnu
```

Aur component me hum **id** aur **name** ko URL se read karenge.

---

## 🧩 Step-by-Step Example

### 1️⃣ App.js

```jsx
import React from "react";
import { BrowserRouter as Router, Route, Routes, Link } from "react-router-dom";
import User from "./User";

function App() {
  return (
    <Router>
      <div style={{ padding: "20px" }}>
        <h2>React useLocation Example</h2>
        <Link to="/user?id=101&name=Vishnu">Go to User Page</Link>

        <Routes>
          <Route path="/user" element={<User />} />
        </Routes>
      </div>
    </Router>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

### 2️⃣ User.js

```jsx
import React from "react";
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";

function User() {
  const location = useLocation();

  // Query params nikalne ke liye
  const queryParams = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
  const id = queryParams.get("id");
  const name = queryParams.get("name");

  return (
    <div>
      <h3>User Page</h3>
      <p>Current Path: {location.pathname}</p>
      <p>User ID: {id}</p>
      <p>User Name: {name}</p>
    </div>
  );
}

export default User;
```

---

## ⚙️ Output:

Jab aap click karoge:

```
Go to User Page
```

to browser URL hoga:

```
http://localhost:3000/user?id=101&name=Vishnu
```

Aur page pe output dikhega:

```
User Page
Current Path: /user
User ID: 101
User Name: Vishnu
```

---

## 💡 Samjho kya hua:

1. `useLocation()` → current URL ka data diya.
2. `location.search` → `?id=101&name=Vishnu` mila.
3. `new URLSearchParams(location.search)` → query params parse karne me help ki.
4. `queryParams.get("id")` → `"101"` mila,
   `queryParams.get("name")` → `"Vishnu"` mila.

---

Agar chaho to mai ek version bana du jisme **navigate karte time state bhi bhej kar** `useLocation` se receive kare (URL me params ke bina)?
e.g. navigate('/user', { state: { id: 101, name: 'Vishnu' } })

==========================================================================================================
React me Protected Route (ya Private Route) ka matlab hota hai —
wo route jise sirf authorized (login kiye hue) user hi access kar sakte hain.
Agar user login nahi hai, to use login page pe redirect kar diya jata hai.

💡 Simple Definition:

Protected Route = Ek aisa route jahan access karne ke liye authentication check lagta hai.

---

## 🧩 Example: Simple Protected Route

### 📁 `App.js`

```jsx
import React from "react";
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, Navigate } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import Dashboard from "./Dashboard";

function App() {
  const isLoggedIn = false; // 👈 yahan hum maan lete hain user login nahi hai

  return (
    <BrowserRouter>
      <Routes>
        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        {/* Protected Route */}
        <Route
          path="/dashboard"
          element={
            isLoggedIn ? <Dashboard /> : <Navigate to="/" replace />
          }
        />
      </Routes>
    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

### 📁 `Home.js`

```jsx
import React from "react";

function Home() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h2>Home Page 🏠</h2>
      <p>Yahan se login karke dashboard ja sakte ho.</p>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Home;
```

---

### 📁 `Dashboard.js`

```jsx
import React from "react";

function Dashboard() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h2>Dashboard 🔒</h2>
      <p>Ye page sirf logged-in users ke liye hai.</p>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Dashboard;
```

---

## ⚙️ Output Behavior

| Case                 | Result                                                  |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `isLoggedIn = false` | Dashboard pe jaane ki koshish karoge → redirect to Home |
| `isLoggedIn = true`  | Dashboard dikhai dega                                   |

---

Bas itna hi logic hota hai Protected Route ka:
👉 “Agar login hai to page dikhao, warna redirect kar do.”

---

Chaho to mai isi example me ek **button ke click pe login karne ka simple version** bhi add kar du (taaki tum switch kar sako Home ↔ Dashboard)?

==========================================================================================================
Bahut accha sawaal 🔥

React me **Protected Route** (ya **Private Route**) ka matlab hota hai —
**wo route jise sirf authorized (login kiye hue) user hi access kar sakte hain.**
Agar user login nahi hai, to use **login page pe redirect** kar diya jata hai.

---

## 💡 Simple Definition:

> Protected Route = Ek aisa route jahan access karne ke liye **authentication check** lagta hai.

---

## 🧠 Real-Life Example:

Jaise koi website hai:

* `/login` — sab dekh sakte hain ✅
* `/dashboard` — sirf login users dekh sakte hain ❌ (agar login nahi ho to redirect to `/login`)

---

## ⚙️ Step-by-Step Example

### 1️⃣ `App.js`

```jsx
import React, { useState } from "react";
import { BrowserRouter as Router, Routes, Route, Navigate } from "react-router-dom";
import Dashboard from "./Dashboard";
import Login from "./Login";
import ProtectedRoute from "./ProtectedRoute";

function App() {
  const [isAuthenticated, setIsAuthenticated] = useState(false);

  return (
    <Router>
      <Routes>
        <Route path="/login" element={<Login setIsAuthenticated={setIsAuthenticated} />} />

        {/* Protected route */}
        <Route
          path="/dashboard"
          element={
            <ProtectedRoute isAuthenticated={isAuthenticated}>
              <Dashboard />
            </ProtectedRoute>
          }
        />
      </Routes>
    </Router>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

### 2️⃣ `ProtectedRoute.js`

```jsx
import React from "react";
import { Navigate } from "react-router-dom";

function ProtectedRoute({ isAuthenticated, children }) {
  if (!isAuthenticated) {
    // Agar login nahi hai to redirect karega /login pe
    return <Navigate to="/login" replace />;
  }
  return children; // agar login hai to content dikhao
}

export default ProtectedRoute;
```

---

### 3️⃣ `Login.js`

```jsx
import React from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Login({ setIsAuthenticated }) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();

  const handleLogin = () => {
    setIsAuthenticated(true); // user ko login kar diya
    navigate("/dashboard"); // redirect to dashboard
  };

  return (
    <div style={{ padding: "20px" }}>
      <h2>Login Page</h2>
      <button onClick={handleLogin}>Login</button>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Login;
```

---

### 4️⃣ `Dashboard.js`

```jsx
import React from "react";

function Dashboard() {
  return (
    <div style={{ padding: "20px" }}>
      <h2>Welcome to Dashboard 🎉</h2>
      <p>Ye page sirf logged-in users ke liye hai.</p>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Dashboard;
```

---

## 🧩 Output Behavior:

| Situation                             | Result                    |
| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| User tries `/dashboard` without login | Redirect → `/login`       |
| User logs in                          | Redirect → `/dashboard`   |
| After login                           | Dashboard content visible |

---

## 🔐 Summary

| Concept             | Description                                            |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Protected Route** | Page only for authenticated users                      |
| **`Navigate`**      | Used to redirect unauthenticated users                 |
| **Usage**           | Wrap protected pages inside `<ProtectedRoute>`         |
| **Control**         | Based on state like `isAuthenticated`, JWT token, etc. |

---

Agar chaho to mai iska **JWT Token based version** bhi bana du (jisme localStorage me token check hota hai)?
Woh real-world production ke jaisa hota hai.

Below are **Q&A-style detailed notes** from the video **“React Router v6 Tutorial – Getting Started / Installation”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)**, exactly like a **teacher asking questions and answering step-by-step**.

---

# React Router v6 – Getting Started (Installation & Basic Routing)

## 1. React Router kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** React Router kya hai?

**Student:**
**React Router** ek **library** hai jo React applications me **multiple pages / navigation** handle karne ke liye use hoti hai.

Normally React ek **Single Page Application (SPA)** hota hai.
Isme page reload nahi hota, sirf **component change hota hai**.

👉 React Router ka kaam:

* Different **URLs** ko **different components** se connect karna.
* Example:

  * `/` → Home Page
  * `/about` → About Page
  * `/contact` → Contact Page

**Important Term:**
**React Router = Navigation system for React apps**

---

## 2. React Router ki zarurat kyun padti hai?

**Teacher:** Agar React me components already hain, to Router kyun chahiye?

**Student:**
Agar Router nahi hoga to:

* Har navigation ke liye **manual logic** likhna padega.
* URL change nahi hoga.
* User directly page access nahi kar payega.

**React Router advantages:**

1️⃣ URL based navigation
2️⃣ Browser history support
3️⃣ Page reload nahi hota
4️⃣ SPA fast rehta hai

---

## 3. React Router ka latest version kya hai?

**Teacher:** React Router ka kaunsa version use ho raha hai?

**Student:**
Is tutorial me **React Router v6** use ho raha hai.

**React Router v6 features:**

* Simple syntax
* Fast routing
* Nested routes support
* Better performance

---

# 4. React Router install kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** React Router install kaise karte hain?

**Student:**
React project me install karne ke liye **npm command** run karte hain.

### Installation Command

```bash
npm install react-router-dom
```

**Explanation:**

| Package          | Meaning                    |
| ---------------- | -------------------------- |
| react-router     | core library               |
| react-router-dom | web browser ke liye router |

👉 Web applications me **react-router-dom** use hota hai.

---

# 5. Example Project Structure

Assume humare paas simple React app hai.

```
src
 ├── App.js
 ├── Home.js
 ├── About.js
 └── index.js
```

Hum do pages banayenge:

1️⃣ Home page
2️⃣ About page

---

# 6. Routing setup ka first step kya hai?

**Teacher:** Router use karne ka first step kya hota hai?

**Student:**
First step hai **BrowserRouter wrap karna**.

React app ko Router ke andar wrap karna padta hai.

### Example

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter } from "react-router-dom";
```

Then wrap inside index.js or App.js.

### Example

```javascript
import React from "react";
import ReactDOM from "react-dom";
import App from "./App";
import { BrowserRouter } from "react-router-dom";

ReactDOM.render(
  <BrowserRouter>
    <App />
  </BrowserRouter>,
  document.getElementById("root")
);
```

**Explanation**

**BrowserRouter**:

* Browser URL ko manage karta hai
* Navigation ko control karta hai

---

# 7. React Router ke important components

**Teacher:** React Router me important components kaunse hote hain?

**Student:**

| Component     | Use                            |
| ------------- | ------------------------------ |
| BrowserRouter | App ko wrap karta hai          |
| Routes        | Routes ko group karta hai      |
| Route         | Specific path define karta hai |

---

# 8. Route ka use kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** Route ka use kaise karte hain?

**Student:**
Route define karta hai **kaunsa component kaunsi URL par open hoga**.

### Example

```javascript
import { Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import About from "./About";

function App() {
  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />
      <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
    </Routes>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

## Explanation

| Path     | Component       |
| -------- | --------------- |
| `/`      | Home component  |
| `/about` | About component |

---

# 9. Home Component Example

**Teacher:** Home component ka example kya hai?

**Student:**

```javascript
function Home() {
  return <h1>Home Page</h1>;
}

export default Home;
```

---

# 10. About Component Example

```javascript
function About() {
  return <h1>About Page</h1>;
}

export default About;
```

---

# 11. Browser me routing kaise check kare?

**Teacher:** Routing kaise test karte hain?

**Student:**
Browser me URL change karke.

Example:

```
http://localhost:3000/
```

Output:

```
Home Page
```

Example:

```
http://localhost:3000/about
```

Output:

```
About Page
```

👉 Page reload nahi hota
👉 Sirf component change hota hai

---

# 12. React Router v6 syntax difference

**Teacher:** React Router v6 me syntax kya change hua hai?

**Student:**

Old version (v5)

```javascript
<Route path="/" component={Home} />
```

New version (v6)

```javascript
<Route path="/" element={<Home />} />
```

**Difference:**

| Version | Syntax    |
| ------- | --------- |
| v5      | component |
| v6      | element   |

---

# 13. Real-world Example

Imagine ek **E-commerce website**.

| URL         | Page          |
| ----------- | ------------- |
| `/`         | Home          |
| `/products` | Product list  |
| `/cart`     | Shopping cart |
| `/login`    | Login page    |

React Router automatically decide karega **kaunsa component show karna hai**.

---

User Browser → URL change

Example:

```
User clicks About
```

Flow:

```
Browser URL change
       ↓
React Router check path
       ↓
Matching Route find
       ↓
Component render
```

```

```

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import About from "./About";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>
      <Routes>
        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

      </Routes>
    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

Routing setup 48 video

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #2 – Links, Navbar, Anchor Tag”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** like **Teacher → Student conversation** with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – Links, Navbar, Anchor Tag

## 1️⃣ React Router me page navigation kaise hota hai?

**Teacher:** React Router me ek page se dusre page par kaise jaate hain?

**Student:**
React Router me navigation ke liye **Link component** use karte hain.

Ye **React Router ka special component** hai jo:

* URL change karta hai
* Page reload nahi karta
* Component render karta hai

**Important Term:**
**Link = React Router navigation component**

---

# 2️⃣ Simple Link kaise banate hain?

**Teacher:** Simple navigation link kaise banate hain?

**Student:**
Sabse pehle **Link import karna padta hai**.

### Step 1: Import Link

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";
```

---

### Step 2: Link create karo

Example:

```javascript
function Home() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1>Home Page</h1>

      <Link to="/about">Go to About Page</Link>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Home;
```

---

### Explanation

| Code          | Meaning                 |
| ------------- | ----------------------- |
| `Link`        | React Router navigation |
| `to="/about"` | kis page par jana hai   |

---

# 3️⃣ Routing setup zaroori hai

**Teacher:** Agar Link use kiya hai to routing bhi karni padegi?

**Student:**
Haan, **Route define karna zaroori hai**.

Example:

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import About from "./About";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>
        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />
        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

# 4️⃣ Navigation ka output kya hoga?

**Teacher:** Agar user link par click kare to kya hoga?

**Student:**

User click karega:

```
Go to About Page
```

Browser URL change hoga:

```
localhost:3000/about
```

React Router render karega:

```
About Component
```

👉 Page reload nahi hoga.

---

# 5️⃣ Navbar kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Navbar kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Navbar (Navigation Bar)** ek menu hota hai jisme multiple links hote hain.

Example website menu:

```
Home | About | Contact
```

React Router me navbar create karne ke liye **multiple Links use karte hain**.

---

# 6️⃣ Navbar ka example

Example:

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Navbar() {
  return (
    <div>

      <Link to="/">Home</Link>

      <Link to="/about">About</Link>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Navbar;
```

---

### Output

```
Home   About
```

User click karega:

```
Home → Home Page
About → About Page
```

---

# 7️⃣ Navbar ko App me kaise use karte hain?

**Teacher:** Navbar ko application me kaise use karte hain?

**Student:**
Navbar ko **App component ke andar use karte hain**.

Example:

```javascript
function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Navbar />

      <Routes>
        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />
        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}
```

---

# 8️⃣ Anchor tag kyun use nahi karte?

**Teacher:** Normal **anchor tag (`<a>`)** kyun use nahi karte?

**Student:**

Example anchor tag:

```html
<a href="/about">About</a>
```

Problem:

❌ Page reload ho jata hai
❌ SPA ka benefit khatam ho jata hai

React application me **Single Page Application (SPA)** hota hai.

Isliye **Link component use karte hain**.

---

# 9️⃣ Link vs Anchor Tag

| Feature                 | Link  | Anchor Tag |
| ----------------------- | ----- | ---------- |
| Page Reload             | ❌ No  | ✔ Yes      |
| SPA Friendly            | ✔ Yes | ❌ No       |
| React Router Compatible | ✔ Yes | ❌ No       |

---

# 10️⃣ Real Example

Imagine ek website:

```
Home | About | Contact
```

Navbar code:

```javascript
function Navbar() {
  return (
    <div>

      <Link to="/">Home</Link>

      <Link to="/about">About</Link>

      <Link to="/contact">Contact</Link>

    </div>
  );
}
```

Routes:

```javascript
<Routes>
  <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />
  <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
  <Route path="/contact" element={<Contact />} />
</Routes>
```

---

# 11️⃣ Navigation Flow

User click karta hai:

```
About
```

Flow:

```
Click Link
     ↓
React Router intercept request
     ↓
URL change
     ↓
Matching Route find
     ↓
Component render
```

---

# 12️⃣ BrowserRouter ka rule

**Teacher:** Router components ko kaha use karna chahiye?

**Student:**
React Router ke components **BrowserRouter ke andar hi kaam karte hain**.

Example:

```javascript
<BrowserRouter>

   <App />

</BrowserRouter>
```

Agar **BrowserRouter ke bahar use karoge** to error aa sakta hai.

---

# 13️⃣ Interview Question

**Teacher:** Interview me agar poocha jaye:

“React me anchor tag kyun use nahi karte?”

**Student Answer:**

Because React applications are **Single Page Applications (SPA)**.

Using **anchor tag reloads the entire page**, while **React Router Link performs client-side navigation without reloading the page**.

---

# 14️⃣ Key Concepts Summary

**Teacher:** Is video me kya kya seekha?

**Student:**

1️⃣ **Link component** navigation ke liye use hota hai
2️⃣ **Navbar multiple links se banta hai**
3️⃣ **BrowserRouter ke andar routing hoti hai**
4️⃣ **Anchor tag page reload karta hai**
5️⃣ **Link SPA navigation provide karta hai**

---

# 15️⃣ Complete Example

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Navbar() {
  return (
    <div>
      <Link to="/">Home</Link>
      <Link to="/about">About</Link>
    </div>
  );
}

function Home() {
  return <h1>Home Page</h1>;
}

function About() {
  return <h1>About Page</h1>;
}

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Navbar />

      <Routes>
        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />
        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

* React Router **Link**
* Navigation **without page reload**
* **Navbar creation**
* **Link vs Anchor Tag**

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #3 – 404 Page Not Found and Redirect”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** as **Teacher → Student conversation**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – 404 Page Not Found & Redirect

---

# 1️⃣ 404 Page Not Found kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** 404 Page Not Found kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**404 Page Not Found** tab show hota hai jab user **aisa URL open karta hai jo application me exist nahi karta**.

Example:

User browser me likhta hai:

```
http://localhost:3000/contact
```

Lekin agar **contact page bana hi nahi hai**, to **404 page show hota hai**.

👉 Isko bolte hain **Page Not Found Error**.

**Important Term:**
**404 Page = Invalid URL par show hone wala error page**

---

# 2️⃣ React Router me 404 page kaise banate hain?

**Teacher:** React Router v6 me 404 page kaise create karte hain?

**Student:**
React Router me **wildcard route (`*`)** use karke 404 page banate hain.

---

# 3️⃣ Step 1 – 404 Component create karo

Sabse pehle ek **component create karte hain**.

Example:

### File: `PageNotFound.js`

```javascript
function PageNotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1>404 Page Not Found</h1>
    </div>
  );
}

export default PageNotFound;
```

---

# 4️⃣ Step 2 – Route define karo

Ab **App.js** me route add karte hain.

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import About from "./About";
import PageNotFound from "./PageNotFound";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />

        <Route path="*" element={<PageNotFound />} />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

# 5️⃣ `*` (Wildcard Route) ka kya matlab hai?

**Teacher:** Route me `*` ka kya meaning hai?

**Student:**

`*` ka matlab hai:

👉 **All unmatched routes**

Matlab agar koi route match nahi hua to:

```
PageNotFound component show hoga
```

Example:

User type kare:

```
localhost:3000/abc
```

Output:

```
404 Page Not Found
```

---

# 6️⃣ 404 page ko better kaise bana sakte hain?

**Teacher:** 404 page ko user-friendly kaise banate hain?

**Student:**
Hum **extra message aur navigation link** add kar sakte hain.

Example:

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";

function PageNotFound() {
  return (
    <div>

      <h1>404 Page Not Found</h1>

      <p>This page does not exist.</p>

      <Link to="/">Go To Home Page</Link>

    </div>
  );
}

export default PageNotFound;
```

---

### Output

```
404 Page Not Found
This page does not exist
Go To Home Page
```

User click karega:

```
Go To Home Page
```

To user **home page par redirect ho jayega**.

---

# 7️⃣ Redirect kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Redirect kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Redirect ka matlab hota hai user ko automatically kisi dusre page par bhejna.**

Example:

User open kare:

```
/contact
```

But contact page exist nahi karta.

To automatically:

```
redirect → home page
```

---

# 8️⃣ React Router v6 me redirect kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** React Router v6 me redirect kaise implement karte hain?

**Student:**
React Router v6 me **Navigate component** use hota hai.

---

# 9️⃣ Redirect Example

Example:

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, Navigate } from "react-router-dom";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />

        <Route path="*" element={<Navigate to="/" />} />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}
```

---

### Explanation

| Code       | Meaning            |
| ---------- | ------------------ |
| `Navigate` | Redirect component |
| `to="/"`   | redirect to home   |

---

### Flow

User open kare:

```
localhost:3000/xyz
```

Router karega:

```
redirect → /
```

Output:

```
Home Page
```

---

# 🔟 404 Page vs Redirect

**Teacher:** 404 page aur redirect me kya difference hai?

**Student:**

| Feature                                 | 404 Page | Redirect |
| --------------------------------------- | -------- | -------- |
| User ko error show hota hai             | ✔        | ❌        |
| Automatically dusre page par bhejta hai | ❌        | ✔        |
| URL change hota hai                     | ❌        | ✔        |

---

# 11️⃣ Real Website Example

Imagine ek website:

```
Home
About
Products
Contact
```

User type kare:

```
mysite.com/abc
```

Website show karegi:

```
404 Page Not Found
```

Ya redirect karegi:

```
Home Page
```

---

# 12️⃣ Navigation Flow

User wrong URL enter karta hai:

```
/xyz
```

React Router flow:

```
URL check
     ↓
Route match nahi hua
     ↓
Wildcard route (*)
     ↓
404 Page show
```

---

# 13️⃣ Important Rules

**Teacher:** Important rules kya yaad rakhne chahiye?

**Student:**

1️⃣ **Wildcard route (`*`) always last me likhte hain**

Wrong order example ❌

```javascript
<Route path="*" element={<PageNotFound />} />
<Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
```

Correct order ✔

```javascript
<Route path="/about" element={<About />} />
<Route path="*" element={<PageNotFound />} />
```

---

# 14️⃣ Complete Example

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Home() {
  return <h1>Home Page</h1>;
}

function About() {
  return <h1>About Page</h1>;
}

function PageNotFound() {
  return (
    <div>

      <h1>404 Page Not Found</h1>

      <Link to="/">Go To Home</Link>

    </div>
  );
}

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />

        <Route path="*" element={<PageNotFound />} />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

# 15️⃣ Key Concepts Summary

**Teacher:** Is video se kya kya learn hua?

**Student:**

1️⃣ **404 Page Not Found** concept
2️⃣ **Wildcard route (`*`)** use
3️⃣ **Custom 404 component create karna**
4️⃣ **Redirect using Navigate**
5️⃣ **Invalid URL handling**

---

✅ After this video you understand:

* React Router **404 error page**
* **Wildcard routing**
* **Redirect concept**
* **Navigate component**

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #4 – Dynamic Routing with Params”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** as **Teacher → Student conversation**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – Dynamic Routing with Params

---

# 1️⃣ Dynamic Routing kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Dynamic Routing kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Dynamic Routing** ka matlab hai **URL me dynamic value pass karna** aur us value ke basis par **different data show karna**.

Example:

```text
/users/anil
/users/peter
/users/russia
```

Yaha **anil, peter, russia** dynamic values hain.

Isliye isko **Dynamic Route** bolte hain.

**Important Term:**
**Dynamic Routing = URL se data pass karna**

---

# 2️⃣ Real-world example kya ho sakta hai?

**Teacher:** Real website me dynamic routing ka use kaha hota hai?

**Student:**
Bahut jagah use hota hai.

Example **E-commerce website**:

```text
/product/101
/product/102
/product/103
```

Yaha:

* 101 → Product 1
* 102 → Product 2
* 103 → Product 3

Ek hi page use hota hai **different product details show karne ke liye**.

---

# 3️⃣ Dynamic Route ka syntax kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** React Router v6 me dynamic route ka syntax kya hota hai?

**Student:**
Dynamic route banane ke liye **colon (`:`)** use karte hain.

Example:

```javascript
<Route path="/user/:name" element={<User />} />
```

Explanation:

| Part     | Meaning           |
| -------- | ----------------- |
| `/user/` | base URL          |
| `:name`  | dynamic parameter |

---

# 4️⃣ Dynamic Route Example

**Teacher:** Ek simple example dikhao.

**Student:**

### App.js

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import User from "./User";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        <Route path="/user/:name" element={<User />} />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

# 5️⃣ Link ke through dynamic route kaise use karte hain?

**Teacher:** User ko dynamic page par kaise bhejte hain?

**Student:**
Hum **Link component** use karte hain.

Example:

### Home.js

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Home() {
  return (
    <div>

      <h1>User List</h1>

      <Link to="/user/anil">Anil</Link>

      <br />

      <Link to="/user/peter">Peter</Link>

      <br />

      <Link to="/user/russia">Russia</Link>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Home;
```

---

### Output

```text
User List

Anil
Peter
Russia
```

User click kare:

```text
Anil
```

URL banega:

```text
localhost:3000/user/anil
```

---

# 6️⃣ URL se parameter kaise read karte hain?

**Teacher:** URL me jo parameter hai usko kaise read karte hain?

**Student:**
React Router me **useParams() hook** use karte hain.

---

# 7️⃣ useParams() ka example

### User.js

```javascript
import { useParams } from "react-router-dom";

function User() {

  const params = useParams();

  return (
    <div>

      <h1>User Name: {params.name}</h1>

    </div>
  );
}

export default User;
```

---

### Output

User click kare:

```text
Peter
```

Browser URL:

```text
/user/peter
```

Output:

```text
User Name: Peter
```

---

# 8️⃣ Parameter ka value kaha se aata hai?

**Teacher:** Parameter ki value kaha se aati hai?

**Student:**
Value **URL se aati hai**.

Example:

```text
/user/anil
```

Router parse karta hai:

```text
name = anil
```

Aur `useParams()` se mil jata hai.

---

# 9️⃣ Multiple parameters bhi use kar sakte hain?

**Teacher:** Kya ek se zyada parameters use kar sakte hain?

**Student:**
Haan.

Example:

```javascript
<Route path="/user/:name/:age" element={<User />} />
```

URL:

```text
/user/anil/25
```

Read using:

```javascript
const params = useParams();
```

Output:

```text
Name: anil
Age: 25
```

---

# 🔟 Example with multiple params

```javascript
import { useParams } from "react-router-dom";

function User() {

  const params = useParams();

  return (
    <div>

      <h1>Name: {params.name}</h1>

      <h2>Age: {params.age}</h2>

    </div>
  );
}

export default User;
```

---

# 11️⃣ Dynamic Routing Flow

User click karta hai:

```text
Anil
```

Flow:

```text
Click Link
      ↓
URL change → /user/anil
      ↓
Route match → /user/:name
      ↓
useParams() read value
      ↓
Component render
```

---

# 12️⃣ Dynamic Routing use cases

**Teacher:** Dynamic routing kaha use hoti hai?

**Student:**

Common use cases:

1️⃣ **User Profile Pages**

```text
/user/101
```

2️⃣ **Product Detail Page**

```text
/product/200
```

3️⃣ **Blog Post**

```text
/blog/react-router
```

4️⃣ **Country Information**

```text
/country/india
```

---

# 13️⃣ Example: Country Detail

Country list:

```text
India
Brazil
USA
Japan
```

User click kare:

```text
India
```

URL:

```text
/country/india
```

Country page show karega:

```text
Country: India
Capital: New Delhi
Population: ...
```

# 14️⃣ Important Rules

**Teacher:** Dynamic routing use karte waqt kya yaad rakhna chahiye?

**Student:**

1️⃣ Parameter ke liye **colon (`:`)** use hota hai

Example:

```javascript
/user/:name
```

2️⃣ Parameter read karne ke liye **useParams() hook**

3️⃣ URL se **dynamic data aata hai**

---

# 15️⃣ Complete Example

### App.js

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import User from "./User";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        <Route path="/user/:name" element={<User />} />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

### Home.js

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Home() {
  return (
    <div>

      <Link to="/user/anil">Anil</Link>

      <Link to="/user/peter">Peter</Link>

      <Link to="/user/russia">Russia</Link>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Home;
```

---

### User.js

```javascript
import { useParams } from "react-router-dom";

function User() {

  const params = useParams();

  return <h1>User Name: {params.name}</h1>;
}

export default User;
```

# React Router v6 – Link and NavLink Style

---

# 1️⃣ Styling ka importance kya hai?

**Teacher:** Website me styling aur design kyun important hai?

**Student:**
Website me **UI (User Interface)** important hota hai. Agar navigation links achhe design ke ho to user ko **website use karna easy lagta hai**.

Example navigation bar:

```
Home   About   Contact
```

Agar styling na ho to links **plain text** lagte hain.

Isliye React Router ke **Link aur NavLink ko style karna important hota hai**.

---

# 2️⃣ React Router me navigation kaise banate hain?

**Teacher:** React Router me navigation kaise banate hain?

**Student:**
Navigation ke liye **Link component** use karte hain.

Example:

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Navbar() {
  return (
    <div>

      <Link to="/">Home</Link>

      <Link to="/about">About</Link>

      <Link to="/contact">Contact</Link>

    </div>
  );
}
```

Output:

```
Home   About   Contact
```

---

# 3️⃣ Link ko style kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** Link component ko CSS style kaise apply karte hain?

**Student:**
Hum **CSS class apply karte hain**.

Example:

```javascript
<Link className="nav-link" to="/">Home</Link>
```

---

### CSS Example

```css
.nav-link{
  color: green;
  text-decoration: none;
  padding: 10px;
}
```

Output:

```
Home   About   Contact
```

Green color links with spacing.

---

# 4️⃣ Navigation bar ka design kaise banate hain?

**Teacher:** Navigation bar ko proper layout kaise dete hain?

**Student:**
Hum **CSS display property** use karte hain.

Example:

```css
.navbar{
  display: flex;
  background-color: lightgray;
  padding: 10px;
}
```

React component:

```javascript
<div className="navbar">

   <Link className="nav-link" to="/">Home</Link>

   <Link className="nav-link" to="/about">About</Link>

   <Link className="nav-link" to="/contact">Contact</Link>

</div>
```

Output:

```
Home | About | Contact
```

Proper navigation bar.

---

# 5️⃣ Text decoration remove kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** Links me jo underline hoti hai usko kaise remove karte hain?

**Student:**
CSS me **text-decoration: none** use karte hain.

Example:

```css
.nav-link{
  text-decoration: none;
}
```

Result:

❌ Home
✔ Home (without underline)

---

# 6️⃣ Hover effect kaise add karte hain?

**Teacher:** Link par hover effect kaise add karte hain?

**Student:**
CSS hover use karte hain.

Example:

```css
.nav-link:hover{
  color: darkgreen;
}
```

Result:

```
Hover → color change
```

User experience better ho jata hai.

---

# 7️⃣ NavLink kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** NavLink kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**NavLink** React Router ka special version hai **Link ka**.

Iska main feature:

👉 **Active link detect karta hai**

Example:

Agar user `/about` page par hai to **About link highlight ho jayega**.

---

# 8️⃣ NavLink ka syntax kya hai?

Example:

```javascript
import { NavLink } from "react-router-dom";

function Navbar() {
  return (
    <div>

      <NavLink to="/">Home</NavLink>

      <NavLink to="/about">About</NavLink>

      <NavLink to="/contact">Contact</NavLink>

    </div>
  );
}
```

---

# 9️⃣ Active link styling kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** Active page ka link highlight kaise karte hain?

**Student:**
NavLink me **active class** automatically apply hoti hai.

Example CSS:

```css
.active{
  color: red;
  font-weight: bold;
}
```

Result:

User `/about` page par hai:

```
Home   About   Contact
       ↑
     highlighted
```

---

# 🔟 NavLink me dynamic style kaise dete hain?

React Router v6 me style function use karte hain.

Example:

```javascript
<NavLink
  to="/about"
  style={({ isActive }) => ({
    color: isActive ? "red" : "black"
  })}
>
  About
</NavLink>
```

Explanation:

| Condition   | Color |
| ----------- | ----- |
| Active page | Red   |
| Other links | Black |

```javascript
      <NavLink to="/contact">Contact</NavLink>

    </div>

  )
}
```

```css
.navbar{
  background: lightgray;
  padding: 10px;
}

  margin: 10px;

```

| -------------- | ----- | ------- |

| Active style   | ❌ No  | ✔ Yes   |
| Menu highlight | ❌ No  | ✔ Yes   |

```
      ↓

      ↓
NavLink active style apply
```

# React Router v6 – Active Link

---

# 1️⃣ Active Link kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Active Link kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Active Link** ka matlab hai **jo navigation link currently open page ko represent karta hai usko highlight karna**.

Example navigation menu:

```text
Home | About | Contact
```

Agar user **About page par hai**, to:

```text
Home | ABOUT | Contact
```

👉 About link **highlight** ho jata hai.

**Important Term:**
**Active Link = Current page ka highlighted navigation link**

---

# 2️⃣ Active Link kyun use karte hain?

**Teacher:** Active link ka use kyun karte hain?

**Student:**
Active link se **user ko pata chalta hai ki wo website ke kis page par hai**.

Example:

Website menu:

```text
Home | About | Contact
```

User click karta hai:

```text
About
```

Navbar show karega:

```text
Home | ABOUT | Contact
```

Isse user ko clear ho jata hai ki **About page open hai**.

---

# 3️⃣ React Router me Active Link kaise banate hain?

**Teacher:** React Router me active link kaise implement karte hain?

**Student:**
React Router me **NavLink component** use karte hain.

`NavLink` automatically **active class apply karta hai**.

---

# 4️⃣ NavLink ka basic syntax

Example:

```javascript
import { NavLink } from "react-router-dom";

function Navbar(){
  return(
    <div>

      <NavLink to="/">Home</NavLink>

      <NavLink to="/about">About</NavLink>

      <NavLink to="/contact">Contact</NavLink>

    </div>
  );
}
```

---

# 5️⃣ Active class kaise add hoti hai?

**Teacher:** Active class automatically kaise add hoti hai?

**Student:**
Jab user kisi link par click karta hai, React Router automatically **`active` class add kar deta hai**.

Example:

User click kare:

```text
/about
```

HTML inspect karoge to milega:

```html
<a class="active">About</a>
```

---

# 6️⃣ Active link ko CSS se style kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** Active link ko style kaise karte hain?

**Student:**
CSS me **`.active` class style karte hain**.

Example:

```css
.active{
  color: red;
  font-weight: bold;
}
```

Result:

```text
Home | ABOUT | Contact
```

About link highlight ho jayega.

---

```javascript
  return(

      <NavLink to="/about">About</NavLink>
```

| ----------- | ----- |

| Normal link | Black |

React Router v6 tutorial in Hindi #7 use SearchParams and setSearchParmas

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #7 – useSearchParams and setSearchParams”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** as **Teacher → Student conversation**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – useSearchParams & setSearchParams

---

# 1️⃣ useSearchParams kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** useSearchParams kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**useSearchParams** React Router ka ek **hook** hai jo **URL query parameters ko read aur update karne ke liye use hota hai**.

Example URL:

```text
/products?price=100
```

Yaha:

```text
price=100
```

ek **query parameter** hai.

**Important Term:**
**Query Parameter = URL ke end me aane wala data**

---

# 2️⃣ Query parameters kya hote hain?

**Teacher:** Query parameters kya hote hain?

**Student:**
Query parameters URL ke end me **question mark (`?`) ke baad likhe jaate hain**.

Example:

```text
/products?price=100&city=delhi
```

Explanation:

| Parameter | Value |
| --------- | ----- |
| price     | 100   |
| city      | delhi |

Ye data **filtering, searching, sorting** ke liye use hota hai.

---

# 3️⃣ Real-world example

**Teacher:** Real website me query params kaha use hote hain?

**Student:**

Example **E-commerce website**:

User filter apply karta hai:

```text
Flipkart / Amazon
```

URL ban sakta hai:

```text
/products?price=500&brand=nike
```

Yaha:

* price filter
* brand filter

URL me store ho jata hai.

---

# 4️⃣ useSearchParams ka syntax kya hai?

**Teacher:** useSearchParams ka syntax kya hai?

**Student:**

```javascript
const [searchParams, setSearchParams] = useSearchParams();
```

Explanation:

| Variable        | Purpose                               |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| searchParams    | query parameters read karne ke liye   |
| setSearchParams | query parameters update karne ke liye |

---

# 5️⃣ useSearchParams import kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** useSearchParams import kaise karte hain?

**Student:**

```javascript
import { useSearchParams } from "react-router-dom";
```

---

# 6️⃣ Query parameter read kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** URL se query parameter ka value kaise read karte hain?

**Student:**
`searchParams.get()` method use karte hain.

Example:

```javascript
const price = searchParams.get("price");
```

Agar URL ho:

```text
/products?price=100
```

Output:

```text
price = 100
```

---

# 7️⃣ Complete example

### Component

```javascript
import { useSearchParams } from "react-router-dom";

function Products(){

  const [searchParams] = useSearchParams();

  const price = searchParams.get("price");

  return(
    <div>

      <h1>Price Filter: {price}</h1>

    </div>
  )
}
```

---

### URL

```text
localhost:3000/products?price=100
```

Output:

```text
Price Filter: 100
```

---

# 8️⃣ Multiple query parameters kaise read karte hain?

**Teacher:** Agar multiple parameters ho to kaise read karte hain?

**Student:**

Example URL:

```text
/products?price=100&city=mumbai
```

Code:

```javascript
const price = searchParams.get("price");
const city = searchParams.get("city");
```

Output:

```text
Price: 100
City: mumbai
```

---

# 9️⃣ setSearchParams kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** setSearchParams kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**setSearchParams** function URL ke query parameters **update karne ke liye use hota hai**.

Example:

```javascript
setSearchParams({ price: 100 });
```

URL automatically change ho jayega:

```text
/products?price=100
```

---

# 🔟 Button click par query params change karna

Example:

```javascript
import { useSearchParams } from "react-router-dom";

function Products(){

  const [searchParams, setSearchParams] = useSearchParams();

  const setPrice = () => {
    setSearchParams({ price: 500 });
  };

  return(
    <div>

      <button onClick={setPrice}>
        Set Price Filter
      </button>

    </div>
  );
}
```

---

### Output

Button click karte hi URL change:

```text
/products?price=500
```

---

# 11️⃣ Input field se search param set karna

Example:

```javascript
import { useSearchParams } from "react-router-dom";

function Search(){

  const [searchParams, setSearchParams] = useSearchParams();

  const handleSearch = (event) => {
    setSearchParams({ text: event.target.value });
  };

  return(

    <div>

      <input
        type="text"
        placeholder="Search..."
        onChange={handleSearch}
      />

    </div>

  );
}
```

---

### User type kare

```text
Laptop
```

URL banega:

```text
/search?text=Laptop
```

---

# 12️⃣ Navigation flow

User type karta hai:

```text
phone
```

Flow:

```text
Input field
     ↓
setSearchParams()
     ↓
URL update
     ↓
Component read using useSearchParams()
```

---

# 13️⃣ useSearchParams use cases

**Teacher:** useSearchParams kaha use hota hai?

**Student:**

Common use cases:

1️⃣ **Search filters**

```text
/products?category=mobile
```

2️⃣ **Sorting**

```text
/products?sort=price
```

3️⃣ **Pagination**

```text
/products?page=2
```

4️⃣ **Location filters**

```text
/jobs?city=pune
```

---

# 14️⃣ Dynamic filtering example

Example:

```text
/products?price=500&brand=nike
```

Output page show karega:

```text
Products under 500
Brand: Nike
```

3️⃣ **searchParams.get() method**
4️⃣ **setSearchParams() function**
5️⃣ **URL filtering system**

If you want, I can also create **next video notes (#8 useNavigate Hook)** in the same **teacher-student style with diagrams and real examples**, which will make **React Router fully clear for interviews and projects.**

==========================================================================================================
Aapne **query params set kar liye (`age` aur `city`) using `setSearchParams`**.
Ab aap pooch rahe ho **“mujhe aage pass karna hai”** — generally iska matlab hota hai:

1️⃣ **Next page par bhejna (navigation ke saath)**
2️⃣ **Dusre component me read karna**

Main dono simple tarike batata hoon.

---

# 1️⃣ Dusre Page me Query Params Read Karna (Most Common)

Agar aapka URL ho:

```
/users?age=25&city=pune
```

To next page par **same `useSearchParams` se read kar sakte ho**.

### Example: `Users.js`

```javascript
import { useSearchParams } from "react-router-dom";

function Users() {

  const [searchParams] = useSearchParams();

  const age = searchParams.get("age");
  const city = searchParams.get("city");

  return (
    <div>
      <h2>User Filter</h2>

      <p>Age: {age}</p>
      <p>City: {city}</p>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Users;
```

Output:

```
Age: 25
City: pune
```

---

# 2️⃣ Next Page par Navigate karke Pass Karna

Agar aap **button click par next page par data bhejna chahte ho**.

Use **`useNavigate`**.

### Update Filter Component

```javascript
import { useSearchParams, useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Filter() {

  const [searchParams, setSearchParams] = useSearchParams();
  const navigate = useNavigate();

  const age = searchParams.get("age") || "";
  const city = searchParams.get("city") || "";

  const goToUsers = () => {
    navigate(`/users?age=${age}&city=${city}`);
  };

  return (
    <div>

      <input
        type="text"
        placeholder="age"
        value={age}
        onChange={(e) =>
          setSearchParams({ age: e.target.value, city })
        }
      />

      <input
        type="text"
        placeholder="city"
        value={city}
        onChange={(e) =>
          setSearchParams({ age, city: e.target.value })
        }
      />

      <button onClick={goToUsers}>
        Go To Users Page
      </button>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Filter;
```

---

# 3️⃣ URL ka Result

User fill kare:

```
age = 25
city = pune
```

URL banega:

```
/users?age=25&city=pune
```

Next page par aap easily read kar sakte ho.

---

# ⚠️ Important Fix in Your Code

Aapke code me ek small issue hai.

City change karte time age remove ho jayega.

Wrong:

```javascript
setSearchParams({ city: e.target.value })
```

Correct:

```javascript
setSearchParams({ age, city: e.target.value })
```

---

# 4️⃣ Real Life Example (Filters)

Example:

```
/products?price=500&brand=nike
```

Filter component:

```
Price input
Brand input
```

Next page:

```
Filtered Products
```

---

✅ **Summary**

| Step            | Work               |
| --------------- | ------------------ |
| setSearchParams | URL me data set    |
| useSearchParams | data read          |
| useNavigate     | next page par pass |

---

💡 Agar chaho to main **React Router ka ek complete real project example (Filter → Product Page → Query Params)** bana ke dikha sakta hoon.
Isse **useSearchParams 100% clear ho jayega.**

React Router v6 tutorial in Hindi #8 Navigation on Click | Navigating programmatically


==========================================================================================================
Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #8 – Navigation on Click | Navigating Programmatically”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** in **Teacher → Student conversation style**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – Navigation on Click (Programmatic Navigation)

---

# 1️⃣ Programmatic Navigation kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Programmatic Navigation kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Programmatic Navigation** ka matlab hai **code ke through page change karna**, bina `<Link>` ya `<NavLink>` use kiye.

Normally navigation aise hota hai:

```text
Click Link → Page Change
```

Lekin kabhi kabhi hume **button click, form submit, ya condition ke basis par page change karna padta hai**.

Uske liye use hota hai:

👉 **useNavigate hook**

---

# 2️⃣ useNavigate kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** useNavigate kya hai?

**Student:**
**useNavigate** React Router ka ek **hook** hai jo **programmatically page navigate karne ke liye use hota hai**.

Example:

```javascript
navigate("/about")
```

Iska matlab:

```text
Go to About Page
```

---

# 3️⃣ useNavigate import kaise karte hain?

**Teacher:** useNavigate ko import kaise karte hain?

**Student:**

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
```

---

# 4️⃣ Basic navigation example

**Teacher:** Button click par page kaise change karte hain?

**Student:**

### Example

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Home() {

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  return (
    <div>

      <button onClick={() => navigate("/about")}>
        Go To About Page
      </button>

    </div>
  );
}

export default Home;
```

---

### Output

User click kare:

```text
Go To About Page
```

Browser open karega:

```text
localhost:3000/about
```

---

# 5️⃣ Multiple buttons navigation example

Example:

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Home(){

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  return(

    <div>

      <button onClick={() => navigate("/about")}>
        Go To About Page
      </button>

      <button onClick={() => navigate("/contact")}>
        Go To Contact Page
      </button>

    </div>

  )
}
```

---

### Output

```text
Go To About Page
Go To Contact Page
```

---

# 6️⃣ Function ke through navigation

**Teacher:** Agar navigation logic function ke andar likhna ho to?

**Student:**

Example:

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Home(){

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  const goToAbout = () => {
    navigate("/about");
  };

  return(

    <div>

      <button onClick={goToAbout}>
        Go To About Page
      </button>

    </div>

  )
}
```

---

# 7️⃣ Conditional navigation

**Teacher:** Condition ke basis par navigation kaise karte hain?

**Student:**

Example:

```javascript
const goToPage = (page) => {

  if(page === "about"){
    navigate("/about");
  } else {
    navigate("/contact");
  }

};
```

Use in button:

```javascript
<button onClick={() => goToPage("about")}>
  About
</button>

<button onClick={() => goToPage("contact")}>
  Contact
</button>
```

---

# 8️⃣ Real-world example

**Teacher:** Real application me useNavigate kaha use hota hai?

**Student:**

Common scenarios:

1️⃣ **Login success**

```text
Login Successful → Dashboard page
```

Example:

```javascript
if(loginSuccess){
  navigate("/dashboard");
}
```

---

2️⃣ **Form submit**

```text
Submit form → Thank You Page
```

Example:

```javascript
navigate("/thank-you");
```

---

3️⃣ **Unauthorized user**

```text
User not logged in → Redirect to Login
```

Example:

```javascript
navigate("/login");
```

---

# 9️⃣ Navigation Flow

User click karta hai:

```text
Button
```

Flow:

```text
Button Click
     ↓
onClick event
     ↓
navigate("/about")
     ↓
URL change
     ↓
About component render
```

---

# 🔟 Link vs useNavigate

**Teacher:** Link aur useNavigate me kya difference hai?

**Student:**

| Feature                 | Link  | useNavigate |
| ----------------------- | ----- | ----------- |
| Navigation              | ✔ Yes | ✔ Yes       |
| Button click navigation | ❌ No  | ✔ Yes       |
| Conditional navigation  | ❌ No  | ✔ Yes       |
| Form submit navigation  | ❌ No  | ✔ Yes       |

---

# 11️⃣ Complete Example

### App.js

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Home from "./Home";
import About from "./About";
import Contact from "./Contact";

function App(){

  return(

    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/" element={<Home />} />

        <Route path="/about" element={<About />} />

        <Route path="/contact" element={<Contact />} />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>

  )

}

export default App;
```

---

### Home.js

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Home(){

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  return(

    <div>

      <button onClick={() => navigate("/about")}>
        About
      </button>

      <button onClick={() => navigate("/contact")}>
        Contact
      </button>

    </div>

  )

}

export default Home;
```

# 1️⃣ Normal Navigation (Link Example)

**Use case:** Menu ya navbar navigation

### Code

```javascript
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Navbar() {
  return (
    <div>
      <Link to="/">Home</Link>
      <br />
      <Link to="/about">About</Link>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Navbar;
```

### Flow

```
User clicks Link
      ↓
URL change
      ↓
Page open
```

Example URL:

```
/about
```

---

# 2️⃣ Button Click Navigation (useNavigate)

**Use case:** Button click par page change karna.

### Code

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Home() {

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  return (
    <div>
      <button onClick={() => navigate("/about")}>
        Go To About Page
      </button>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Home;
```

### Flow

```
Button Click
     ↓
navigate("/about")
     ↓
About page open
```

---

# 3️⃣ Conditional Navigation Example

**Use case:** Login success / failure.

### Code

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function Login() {

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  const loginUser = () => {

    const isLoggedIn = true;

    if (isLoggedIn) {
      navigate("/dashboard");
    } else {
      navigate("/login");
    }

  };

  return (
    <div>
      <button onClick={loginUser}>
        Login
      </button>
    </div>
  );
}

export default Login;
```

### Flow

```
Login Button Click
        ↓
Check Condition
        ↓
navigate("/dashboard")
```

---

# 4️⃣ Form Submit Navigation

**Use case:** Form submit hone ke baad next page open.

### Code

```javascript
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

function ContactForm() {

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  const handleSubmit = (e) => {
    e.preventDefault();

    // form submit logic

    navigate("/thank-you");
  };

  return (
    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>

      <input type="text" placeholder="Name" />

      <button type="submit">
        Submit
      </button>

    </form>
  );
}

export default ContactForm;
```

### Flow

```
Form Submit
     ↓
handleSubmit()
     ↓
navigate("/thank-you")
```

---

# 5️⃣ Real Website Example

### Navbar (Link)

```
Home | Products | Contact
```

### Button Navigation (useNavigate)

```
Add to Cart → Cart Page
Login → Dashboard
Submit Form → Thank You Page
```

---

# 🧠 Simple Rule (Very Important)

| Situation               | Use             |
| ----------------------- | --------------- |
| Menu navigation         | **Link**        |
| Button click navigation | **useNavigate** |
| Login redirect          | **useNavigate** |
| Form submit redirect    | **useNavigate** |

---

✅ **Short Memory Trick**

```
Menu → Link
Logic / Button / Condition → useNavigate
```

React Router v6 tutorial in Hindi #9 Nested Routing

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #9 – Nested Routing”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** as **Teacher → Student conversation**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – Nested Routing

---

# 1️⃣ Nested Routing kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Nested Routing kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Nested Routing** ka matlab hai **ek route ke andar aur routes banana**.

Matlab:

```
Main Route
   ↓
Child Routes
```

Example:

```
/contact
/contact/company
/contact/channel
/contact/other
```

Yaha:

* **/contact** → Parent Route
* **company, channel, other** → Child Routes

**Important Term:**
**Nested Routing = Parent route ke andar child routes**

---

# 2️⃣ Real-world example

**Teacher:** Real website me nested routing kaha use hota hai?

**Student:**

Example **Dashboard website**:

```
/dashboard
/dashboard/profile
/dashboard/settings
/dashboard/orders
```

Yaha:

* Dashboard → Parent page
* Profile / Settings / Orders → Child pages

---

# 3️⃣ Nested routing ka concept kaise samjhe?

**Teacher:** Simple example se samjhao.

**Student:**

Imagine ek **Contact page** hai.

Contact page ke andar 3 sections hain:

```
Contact
 ├ Company
 ├ Channel
 └ Other
```

URLs:

```
/contact/company
/contact/channel
/contact/other
```

Ye **nested routing** hai.

---

# 4️⃣ Nested routing banane ke liye kya chahiye?

**Teacher:** Nested routing banane ke liye kya required hai?

**Student:**

React Router me nested routing ke liye 3 cheeze chahiye:

1️⃣ **Parent Route**
2️⃣ **Child Routes**
3️⃣ **Outlet component**

---

# 5️⃣ Parent Route ka example

### App.js

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Contact from "./Contact";
import Company from "./Company";
import Channel from "./Channel";

function App() {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/contact" element={<Contact />}>

          <Route path="company" element={<Company />} />

          <Route path="channel" element={<Channel />} />

        </Route>

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>
  );
}

export default App;
```

---

# 6️⃣ URL structure

| URL                | Page         |
| ------------------ | ------------ |
| `/contact`         | Contact Page |
| `/contact/company` | Company Page |
| `/contact/channel` | Channel Page |

---

# 7️⃣ Outlet component kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Outlet kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Outlet ek placeholder component hai jaha child routes render hote hain.**

Example:

Parent component:

```javascript
<Contact />
```

Child pages **Outlet ke andar render hote hain**.

---

# 8️⃣ Outlet ka example

### Contact.js

```javascript
import { Outlet, Link } from "react-router-dom";

function Contact(){

  return(
    <div>

      <h1>Contact Page</h1>

      <Link to="company">Company</Link>

      <Link to="channel">Channel</Link>

      <Link to="other">Other</Link>

      <Outlet />

    </div>
  )

}

export default Contact;
```

---

# 9️⃣ Outlet ka role

Flow:

```
Contact Page
     ↓
Outlet
     ↓
Company / Channel / Other component
```

---

# 🔟 Child Component example

### Company.js

```javascript
function Company(){

  return(

    <div>

      <h2>This is Company Page</h2>

    </div>

  )

}

export default Company;
```

---

### Channel.js

```javascript
function Channel(){

  return(

    <div>

      <h2>This is Channel Page</h2>

    </div>

  )

}

export default Channel;
```

---

# 11️⃣ Navigation Example

User click karta hai:

```
Contact → Company
```

URL change:

```
/contact/company
```

Output:

```
Contact Page
This is Company Page
```

---

# 12️⃣ Navigation Flow

```
User clicks Company
        ↓
URL → /contact/company
        ↓
Parent Route → /contact
        ↓
Child Route → company
        ↓
Outlet render
```

---

# 13️⃣ Folder Structure Example

```
src
 ├ App.js
 ├ Contact.js
 ├ Company.js
 ├ Channel.js
 └ Other.js
```

---

# 14️⃣ Real project example

Imagine ek **Admin Dashboard**:

```
/admin
/admin/users
/admin/orders
/admin/products
```

Admin page me menu hoga:

```
Users
Orders
Products
```

Child pages **Outlet me render honge**.

---

# 15️⃣ Nested Routing ka benefit

**Teacher:** Nested routing ka benefit kya hai?

**Student:**

✔ Code **clean rehta hai**
✔ UI **structured rehta hai**
✔ Layout **reuse ho sakta hai**
✔ Complex apps me useful

Example:

```
Dashboard layout
   ↓
Child pages inside layout
```

---

# 16️⃣ Complete Example

### App.js

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Contact from "./Contact";
import Company from "./Company";
import Channel from "./Channel";

function App() {

  return(

    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/contact" element={<Contact />}>

          <Route path="company" element={<Company />} />

          <Route path="channel" element={<Channel />} />

        </Route>

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>

  )

}

export default App;
```

---

# 17️⃣ Key Concepts Summary

**Teacher:** Is video me kya kya seekha?

**Student:**

1️⃣ **Nested Routing concept**
2️⃣ **Parent and Child routes**
3️⃣ **Outlet component**
4️⃣ **Nested URL structure**
5️⃣ **Contact → Company / Channel example**

---

✅ After this video you understand:

* **Nested Routing**
* **Outlet component**
* **Parent & Child routes**
* **Nested URL structure**

---

If you want, I can also make the **next video notes (#10 React Router Layout Routing)** which is **very important for real React projects**, and it will make **React Router fully clear in one roadmap.**

==========================================================================================================
Bahut acha question hai 👍
**`Outlet` automatic kaise kaam karta hai** ye samajhna React Router ka important concept hai.

Main simple flow me samjhata hoon.

---

# 1️⃣ Pehle Router kya karta hai

Jab browser me URL open hota hai:

```
/users
```

React Router kya karta hai?

1️⃣ URL read karta hai
2️⃣ Route match karta hai
3️⃣ Component render karta hai

---

# 2️⃣ Nested routing example

Routes:

```jsx
<Route path="/" element={<Layout />}>

  <Route path="dashboard" element={<Dashboard />} />

  <Route path="users" element={<Users />} />

  <Route path="orders" element={<Orders />} />

</Route>
```

---

# 3️⃣ Jab user `/users` open karta hai

Browser URL:

```
http://localhost:3000/users
```

React Router matching karta hai:

```
Parent Route  → /
Child Route   → users
```

Matched components:

```
Layout
Users
```

---

# 4️⃣ Layout component render hota hai

Layout component:

```jsx
function Layout(){
  return(
    <>
      <Navbar />
      <Sidebar />
      <Outlet />
    </>
  )
}
```

Ab React Router kya karta hai:

```
Layout render
   ↓
Navbar
Sidebar
Outlet
```

---

# 5️⃣ Outlet ka role

`Outlet` ek **placeholder** hai.

React Router internally bolta hai:

```
Matched child route = Users
```

Isliye:

```
Outlet → Users component render karega
```

Final UI:

```
Navbar
Sidebar
--------
Users Page
```

---

# 6️⃣ Visual flow

```
URL → /users
      ↓
Route match
      ↓
Parent = Layout
Child = Users
      ↓
Render Layout
      ↓
Outlet replace with Users
```

---

# 7️⃣ Final output example

Layout:

```
-------------------
Navbar
-------------------
Sidebar | Users Page
-------------------
```

---

# 8️⃣ Agar `/orders` open kare

URL:

```
/orders
```

Outlet render karega:

```
Orders component
```

UI:

```
Navbar
Sidebar
Orders Page
```

---

# 9️⃣ Simple analogy

`Outlet` ko aise samjho:

```
Layout = Frame
Outlet = Empty Space
```

Example:

```
Frame
  ↓
Content change hota hai
```

---

# 🔟 Real industry reason

Isliye companies use karti hain:

```
Navbar
Sidebar
Footer
```

Same rehta hai.

Sirf:

```
Outlet content change hota hai
```

---

✅ **Short answer**

`Outlet` automatic kaam karta hai because **React Router internally matched child route component ko Outlet me inject karta hai.**

---

💡 Agar chaho to main tumhe **React Router ka internal working diagram (kaise match karta hai routes)** bhi samjha sakta hoon — jo **senior React developers ko pata hota hai**.

==========================================================================================================
React Router v6 tutorial in Hindi #10 useLocation Hooks | use Location

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #10 – useLocation Hook”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** as **Teacher → Student conversation**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – useLocation Hook

---

# 1️⃣ useLocation Hook kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** useLocation hook kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**useLocation** React Router ka ek **hook** hai jo current **URL ki information** deta hai.

Is hook se hume pata chalta hai:

* current **path**
* **query parameters**
* **state data**
* **hash**
* **unique key**

Simple words me:

👉 **useLocation = current page ki URL details lene ka hook**

---

# 2️⃣ useLocation kab use karte hain?

**Teacher:** useLocation kab use hota hai?

**Student:**
Jab hume **current route ki information check karni ho** tab useLocation use karte hain.

Example:

* current page check karna
* query params read karna
* state data read karna
* analytics tracking

---

# 3️⃣ useLocation ka syntax

**Teacher:** useLocation ka syntax kya hai?

**Student:**

```javascript
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";

const location = useLocation();
```

Ab `location` object me **URL related data milta hai**.

---

# 4️⃣ useLocation kya return karta hai?

**Teacher:** useLocation se kya kya data milta hai?

**Student:**
useLocation ek **location object** return karta hai.

Example:

```javascript
console.log(location)
```

Output:

```text
{
  pathname: "/about",
  search: "",
  hash: "",
  state: null,
  key: "abc123"
}
```

---

# 5️⃣ location object properties

| Property     | Meaning                 |
| ------------ | ----------------------- |
| **pathname** | current URL path        |
| **search**   | query parameters        |
| **hash**     | hash value              |
| **state**    | route state data        |
| **key**      | unique route identifier |

---

# 6️⃣ Example – pathname use karna

### Code

```javascript
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";

function Example(){

  const location = useLocation();

  return(
    <h1>Current Path: {location.pathname}</h1>
  )
}
```

---

### URL

```text
/about
```

Output:

```text
Current Path: /about
```

---

# 7️⃣ location.search kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** location.search kya hota hai?

**Student:**
Ye **query parameters** deta hai.

Example URL:

```text
/products?price=100
```

Code:

```javascript
const location = useLocation();

console.log(location.search);
```

Output:

```text
?price=100
```

---

# 8️⃣ location.state kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** location.state kya hota hai?

**Student:**
`state` me **data pass kar sakte hain ek route se dusre route me**.

Example:

```javascript
<Link
  to="/about"
  state={{ name: "Anil" }}
>
  Go to About
</Link>
```

---

# 9️⃣ state ko kaise read karte hain?

About page me:

```javascript
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";

function About(){

  const location = useLocation();

  return(
    <h1>Name: {location.state.name}</h1>
  )

}
```

Output:

```text
Name: Anil
```

---

# 🔟 Navigation example

User click karta hai:

```text
Go to About
```

Flow:

```text
Link click
   ↓
State pass
   ↓
URL change
   ↓
useLocation read state
```

---

# 11️⃣ location.key kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** location.key kya hota hai?

**Student:**
`key` ek **unique identifier** hota hai jo har navigation ke time generate hota hai.

Example:

```text
key: "x93ks"
```

Ye mostly **internal use** ke liye hota hai.

---

# 12️⃣ Real use case

Example **analytics tracking**

```javascript
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";

function Tracker(){

  const location = useLocation();

  useEffect(()=>{
     console.log("Page visited:", location.pathname);
  },[location]);

}
```

Isse pata chalta hai user **kaunsa page open kar raha hai**.

---

# 13️⃣ Real world example

Imagine **E-commerce site**

User open karta hai:

```text
/products
```

useLocation se track kar sakte hain:

```text
User visited Products page
```

---

# 14️⃣ Navigation flow

```text
User click route
      ↓
URL change
      ↓
useLocation update
      ↓
Component render
```

---

# 15️⃣ Key concepts summary

**Teacher:** Is video me kya kya seekha?

**Student:**

1️⃣ **useLocation hook**
2️⃣ **location object**
3️⃣ **pathname property**
4️⃣ **search query parameters**
5️⃣ **state data passing**

---

# 16️⃣ Complete Example

```javascript
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";

function Page(){

  const location = useLocation();

  return(

    <div>

      <h1>Path: {location.pathname}</h1>

      <h2>Query: {location.search}</h2>

    </div>

  )

}
```

* **Location object**
* **Reading current route**
* **Passing data using state**

React Router v6 tutorial in Hindi #11 Protected Route

Below are **detailed Q&A-style notes** for the video:
**“React Router v6 Tutorial #11 – Protected Route”** explained in **simple Hindi-style English (Hinglish)** as a **Teacher → Student conversation**, step-by-step with examples.

---

# React Router v6 – Protected Route

---

# 1️⃣ Protected Route kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Protected Route kya hota hai?

**Student:**
**Protected Route** ek **logical implementation** hota hai jisme kuch pages **sirf logged-in users ke liye accessible hote hain**.

Matlab:

```text
Agar user login nahi hai
→ page access nahi kar sakta
→ login page par redirect ho jayega
```

**Important Term:**
**Protected Route = login ke bina access na hone wala route**

---

# 2️⃣ Real-world example

**Teacher:** Real websites me protected routes kaha use hote hain?

**Student:**

Example:

| Website     | Protected Page    |
| ----------- | ----------------- |
| Banking app | Account dashboard |
| E-commerce  | Order history     |
| Admin panel | Admin dashboard   |

Example URL:

```text
/dashboard
/orders
/profile
```

Ye pages **login ke bina open nahi honge**.

---

# 3️⃣ Protected Route ka logic kya hota hai?

**Teacher:** Protected route ka logic kaise kaam karta hai?

**Student:**
Protected route me **condition check hoti hai**.

Example logic:

```text
If user logged in
   → show page
Else
   → redirect to login
```

---

# 4️⃣ Login page banana

Sabse pehle ek **Login Page** banate hain.

### Example: Login.js

```javascript
function Login(){

  return(
    <div>

      <h1>Login Page</h1>

      <button>Login</button>

    </div>
  )

}

export default Login;
```

---

# 5️⃣ Login state kaise store karte hain?

**Teacher:** Login status kaise store karte hain?

**Student:**
Is example me **localStorage** use kiya gaya hai.

Example:

```javascript
localStorage.setItem("login", true);
```

Ye browser me login status store karta hai.

---

# 6️⃣ Login button ka example

```javascript
function Login(){

  const handleLogin = () => {

    localStorage.setItem("login", true);

  }

  return(

    <div>

      <button onClick={handleLogin}>
        Login
      </button>

    </div>

  )

}
```

---

# 7️⃣ ProtectedRoute component banana

Ab ek **ProtectedRoute component** banate hain.

### Example

```javascript
import { Navigate } from "react-router-dom";

function ProtectedRoute({children}){

  const login = localStorage.getItem("login");

  if(!login){
    return <Navigate to="/login" />
  }

  return children;

}

export default ProtectedRoute;
```

---

# 8️⃣ ProtectedRoute ka explanation

| Code                          | Meaning                |
| ----------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| localStorage.getItem("login") | check login status     |
| !login                        | agar login nahi hai    |
| Navigate                      | redirect to login page |
| children                      | protected component    |

---

# 9️⃣ Protected route use karna

### App.js

```javascript
import ProtectedRoute from "./ProtectedRoute";

<Route
  path="/about"
  element={
    <ProtectedRoute>
      <About />
    </ProtectedRoute>
  }
/>
```

---

# 🔟 Flow samjho

User open karta hai:

```text
/about
```

React Router check karega:

```text
Is user logged in?
```

Case 1:

```text
Yes
→ About page open
```

Case 2:

```text
No
→ Redirect to /login
```

---

# 11️⃣ Navigation flow diagram

```text
User open /about
        ↓
ProtectedRoute check
        ↓
Login status
   ↓         ↓
Yes         No
 ↓           ↓
About       Redirect → Login
```

---

# 12️⃣ Logout example

Logout karne ke liye:

```javascript
localStorage.removeItem("login");
```

User logout hone ke baad protected pages access nahi kar payega.

---

# 13️⃣ Complete example

### App.js

```javascript
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
import Login from "./Login";
import About from "./About";
import ProtectedRoute from "./ProtectedRoute";

function App(){

  return(

    <BrowserRouter>

      <Routes>

        <Route path="/login" element={<Login />} />

        <Route
          path="/about"
          element={
            <ProtectedRoute>
              <About />
            </ProtectedRoute>
          }
        />

      </Routes>

    </BrowserRouter>

  )

}

export default App;
```

---

# 14️⃣ Real project example

Imagine **Admin Panel**

```text
/admin/dashboard
/admin/users
/admin/orders
```

Logic:

```text
If admin logged in
 → allow access
Else
 → redirect to login
```

---

# 15️⃣ Important interview point

**Teacher:** React Router me protected route built-in feature hai kya?

**Student:**
❌ **No**

Protected route **React Router ka built-in feature nahi hai**.

Ye **developer ka custom logic hota hai**.

**Teacher:** Is video me kya seekha?

**Student:**

Koi problem nahi 👍
Main **bahut simple step-by-step flow me samjhata hoon** ki ye code kaise kaam karta hai.

---

# 1️⃣ Sabse pehle problem samjho

Maan lo tumhare app me ek page hai:

```
/about
```

Tum chahte ho:

```
Agar user login hai → About page open ho
Agar login nahi hai → Login page par bhej do
```

Isko hi **Protected Route** bolte hain.

---

# 2️⃣ Login status kaha store hai

Tumhare code me login status store ho raha hai:

```javascript
localStorage.setItem("login", true)
```

Browser me store hota hai.

Example:

```
localStorage
  login = true
```

---

# 3️⃣ ProtectedRoute component kya karta hai

Code:

```javascript
function ProtectedRoute({children}){

  const login = localStorage.getItem("login");

  if(!login){
    return <Navigate to="/login" />
  }

  return children;

}
```

Iska matlab:

### Step 1

Login check karo

```javascript
const login = localStorage.getItem("login");
```

---

### Step 2

Agar login **nahi hai**

```javascript
if(!login)
```

to user ko bhej do:

```javascript
return <Navigate to="/login" />
```

Matlab:

```
redirect → login page
```

---

### Step 3

Agar login **hai**

```javascript
return children
```

Matlab:

```
jo page diya hai use show karo
```

---

# 4️⃣ App.js me kya ho raha hai

Code:

```javascript
<Route
  path="/about"
  element={
    <ProtectedRoute>
      <About />
    </ProtectedRoute>
  }
/>
```

Iska matlab:

```
/about open karne se pehle
ProtectedRoute check karo
```

---

# 5️⃣ Flow samjho

User open karta hai:

```
http://localhost:3000/about
```

React Router karega:

```
ProtectedRoute run
```

---

## Case 1️⃣ Login nahi hai

```
localStorage login = null
```

Condition:

```
if(!login) true
```

Result:

```
Navigate → /login
```

User ko dikhega:

```
Login Page
```

---

## Case 2️⃣ Login hai

```
localStorage login = true
```

Condition:

```
if(!login) false
```

Result:

```
return children
```

Children kya hai?

```
<About />
```

User ko dikhega:

```
About Page
```

---

# 6️⃣ Visual flow

```
User → /about
        ↓
ProtectedRoute
        ↓
Check login
    ↓        ↓
   No       Yes
    ↓        ↓
 /login    About Page
```

---

# 7️⃣ children kya hai?

Yaha:

```javascript
<ProtectedRoute>
   <About />
</ProtectedRoute>
```

Iska matlab:

```
children = <About />
```

Isliye code:

```javascript
return children
```

Matlab:

```
About page render karo
```

---

# 8️⃣ Real life example

Maan lo:

```
Bank app
```

Routes:

```
/login
/dashboard
```

Rule:

```
login nahi → dashboard nahi dekh sakte
```

To:

```
dashboard = protected route
```

---

# 9️⃣ Short version

ProtectedRoute code ka meaning:

```
Agar login nahi
   → login page bhejo
Agar login hai
   → page open karo
```

---

✅ **Sabse simple line**

```
ProtectedRoute = gatekeeper
```

```
Login hai → andar jao
Login nahi → login karo
```
